T2 failures and QA findings, by area and priority (docs/PRIORITIES.md). The area agent owns its tickets; when one is
fixed, mark it `[x]` with the commit, and move the test back to T1 if it is now deterministic and fast
(docs/TESTS.md).

## From the user, 2026-11-02 (v0.4.19, macOS)
- [x] (psui 11-03 00:10, fixed by "psui: VAR-AUTOCOMPLETE-ENTER …": (1) an open feature dialog kept the variables of
  the regeneration before it opened, so a dialog opened before the one defining `#wall_thickness` finished listed only
  "New variable…" (now they follow every regeneration); (2) in a sketch dimension box Enter committed the typed `#wall`
  (live 11-02: Enter inserts the highlighted entry and the box stays; only when that changes nothing, `#var` typed
  whole, does it commit); (3) in a dialog field the inserted text came up selected (live: the caret after it, and the
  next Enter accepts the dialog). T1 dialog_field_enter_inserts_the_highlighted_variable,
  dimension_box_enter_inserts_the_highlighted_variable, a_first_click_into_a_number_field_selects_all. qa's first-click
  and Tab findings are an Xvfb artifact: a window that was never activated (no window manager) gets no focus events,
  so select-on-focus never runs; with `xdotool windowfocus` (or any real desktop) one click selects all as live (Tab
  goes through the same select-on-focus). psui 11-03 12:20, live's recording extrude-depth-variable-enter-twice
  corrects (3): the inserted text isn't applied (Undo stays off); the next Enter applies it (selected, an undo step,
  the dialog stays) and the one after accepts; an Enter whose entry changes nothing (`#width`, `1 in`) applies at once.)
  P1 VAR-AUTOCOMPLETE-ENTER (psui, user 11-02 23:30, v0.4.2x on macOS) in a dialog's number field, typing
  `#wall` then Enter doesn't take the first match `#wall_thickness`; live highlights the first match as you type and
  Enter inserts it. Reproduce side by side with live (which field, what Enter does in ours), fix, and make it the
  first scenario of the interaction suite (docs/PARITY-SUITES.md).
  qa 11-02 23:52 (**corrects qa's 23:15 / 23:22 notes, withdrawn**): those runs had the app window **not focused** (Xvfb
  without a window manager: xdotool clicks don't give the X window focus), and unfocused our number fields put the caret
  at the click and draw no selection. With `xdotool windowfocus <win>` first, **green 6b4704f2 and the v0.4.22 release
  both do what live does on Linux**: one click on Depth selects all of "10 mm" (highlighted), `#wall` → "(x)
  #wall_thickness 2 mm" highlighted / "(x) New variable…", Enter → "#wall_thickness" (selected), the preview at 2 mm;
  typed at full speed with Enter straight after, the same (qa-scratch/vd-focused.png, v22-focused.png, v22-fast.png;
  start document qa-scratch/tickets/varac/vardoc.noshape, bin/vardoc.py). **So it doesn't reproduce on Linux: it looks
  macOS-only.** Live checked today (qa's tab, Part Studio "qa parity vars" in "noshape mm test", qa-scratch/livek/t-var.mjs,
  t-tab.mjs): one click → selectionStart 0 / End 5 of "10 mm"; `#wall` → "*#wall_thickness 2 mm" / "New variable…";
  Enter → "#wall_thickness"; Tab order from the region field: end-type select, direction button, Depth (all selected), ±
  toggle. macOS suspects for psui: `#` typed with Option+3 (UK / many European layouts: the key arrives with ⌥ held or
  through the input method's insertText, so the list may not open or not get the key), or Return arriving as a
  different key / through the IME's commit before our list sees it. Needs a run on a Mac (or psui's UI driver on macOS
  CI) with a non-US layout. Scenario qa-scratch/ui-parity/extrude-depth-variable-click-once.json.
  qa 11-03: **another path that gives exactly the user's symptom, on Linux too: P1 DIALOG-SWITCH-DISCARDS-NEW** (psui
  section): a new Variable's dialog left open after its first Enter is thrown away when the user double-clicks the
  Extrude to use it (live keeps the variable), so #wall then finds nothing. (Note: in live too a Variable made after
  Extrude 1 sits below it, and a feature sees only variables above it.)
- [ ] MACOS-GUARD-LOCK (core, CI 41351354303 on agent/ci2 76a61696, macOS arm64 Test, once): the model tests' `all`
  ended with "noshape: the kernel crashed holding a lock; not recoverable" (SIGSEGV) in the custom-feature tests,
  just after `a_cancelled_custom_feature_stops_soon_and_the_studio_is_as_before`. In flight then:
  `ops_and_evaluations_are_the_same_on_many_threads` (the ops harness on 4 threads), `ops_and_evaluations_run_in_
  custom_features`, `part_studio_reference_swept_body_query`. On macOS the mutex counting is off, so that message
  means a fault inside a LockedSection (an OcShape cache's call_once body, mirror_copy, mesh / XDE locks). No stack
  then: a fatal guard exit now always prints its backtrace (Linux and macOS; the unwinder loaded at install, so it
  doesn't allocate under malloc's lock). Linux: 60 runs of custom_features at 8 threads clean; 200 of the in-flight
  set with NOSHAPE_TEST_THREADS=12 running (vibe4 ~/agents/logs/racestress2.log). Next: the stack, when it recurs.
  core 11-02 16:10: a likely cause, fixed in 4099de0f (CRASH-1388): a fault the guard recovered past run_budgeted left
  its Deadline (on the dead frame) as the thread's t_deadline on every platform (macOS's budget is the deadline alone),
  and the next deadline check wrote into whatever the stack held there: a lock's state among them. Open until CI
  shows the custom-feature tests clean on macOS for a few runs. 18:20: CI 41471129554 (5df5c21b) and 41485509681
  (1bd31f72), both after the fix, all green on macOS arm64.
- [x] P0 SHELL-VOIDS-WINDOWS (core, CI a8c535aa / 67e44bb7): `shell::a_void_waits_for_live` failed on Windows only.
  Cause: the test switched the void gate on with `std::env::set_var`, which on Windows changes the process
  environment but not the MSVC CRT's copy that the kernel's C++ `getenv` reads (taken at start-up); Linux / macOS
  share one. The gate is now a switch (`noshape_kernel::set_shell_voids`, thread-local, NOSHAPE_SHELL_VOIDS read
  once as its default) the C++ side asks through the bridge; the test uses it. Default (off) unchanged everywhere.
  Same pattern, not failing today (owners note): kernel tests boolean_budget.rs / bool_patterns.rs (NOSHAPE_BOOLEAN_
  MEMORY_MB, NOSHAPE_BOP_PARALLEL, NOSHAPE_KERNEL_PROFILE, NOSHAPE_BOOL_NO_SCALE), band_mesh.rs, rust_mesh.rs.
  (11-02 03:45: the switch is gone with the void walls: live refuses every Shell of a solid with a void (1757), so
  such a Shell now fails at once; the test is `shell::a_solid_with_a_void_doesnt_shell`.)
- [x] verified 7b11ede71 (qa 11-02 00:52, qa-scratch/bin/splitnames.py: a Ø20 × 30 cylinder split by Right then Front into 4 quarters, Chamfer 0.8 mm of one quarter's x = 0 cut face: **v0.4.19 chamfers two parts** (Part 1 and Part 3, both 2331.1635 / 9 faces), **green 4a0fc474 only Part 1** (2331.1635 / 9; the other three 2356.1945 / 5)) P0 SPLIT-PIECE-NAMES (core): a revolved body split by two planes into 3 parts; Chamfer "Face of Split 1" (one
  face picked on one part, 0.8 mm, tangent propagation) chamfered the matching face of all three. Cause: OCCT's
  splitter leaves a cut face shared by the two pieces (one face in both solids), so both pieces' faces got one name and
  the reference resolved on every part carrying it. Fixed: `split_solids(.., apart)` / `name_pieces_apart` name a face
  the pieces share apart per piece (`TopoName::Split` under the dividing feature) for every op whose result becomes
  several bodies (Split, booleans into pieces, new bodies, surface results, rips, ribs, the FS host's bodies); a name
  saved before still finds every piece's face through `descends_from` (old documents regenerate as they did: 150 of
  qa's / qa5's / the profile documents identical before and after: part volumes, face / edge counts, statuses). T1
  `split_names::*` (7, five fail on the previous main): pieces name apart (side by side and stacked), Chamfer /
  Fillet / sketch plane / Shell / mate connector / Draft / Extrude up to face on one piece's face change or take
  that piece only, part pattern / mirror / subtraction in two / repeated splits name apart, the old shared name finds
  both faces.

  (CancelIndicator: op_boolean's builds, op_section), so std's collision checks (MIRROR-HANG-402) and Booleans stop
  on macOS / Windows too. Still unbounded off Linux: fillet / chamfer (BRepFilletAPI), the offset and slabs of Shell
  (quick_boolean builds without the indicator), Draft, defeaturing: their builds need the indicator's range passed
  (Build(range) where OCCT takes one) or a check between steps. Test: kernel `a_section_under_a_call_budget_gives_up`
  (all three in CI); build with CXXFLAGS=-DNOSHAPE_PORTABLE_BUDGET to try the deadline path on Linux.

OCCT unifies the coplanar pieces where the mirrored half meets the original; the Rust result keeps a split face (topology differs from live, which merges same-domain faces), and downstream edge-based features change. A minimal box-cup mirror (bin/mirfuse.py) unifies the same in both. **Minimal repro (qa 00:47): tickets/rust-mirror-min.noshape** = that document cut to Sketch 1 / Extrude 1 / Shell 1 / Mirror 1: OCCT 19 faces, Rust 20; the face lists differ only in the **hole's cylinder wall** (axis (20, 20), across the mirror plane z = 0): OCCT one cylinder face 1507.964 mm² centred z 0, Rust **two cylinder faces 753.982 mm² each** (centres z −10 and +10). So the Rust fuse unifies coplanar planes but not same-domain cylinders split by the mirror plane; the later Chamfer then sees the seam edge. **Everyday repro (qa 00:50): tickets/mirhole.noshape** (tickets/mirhole.py): a 40×20×10 plate on Top with a through Ø10 hole, Part mirror across Top, Add → OCCT 7 faces (hole wall one 628.319 mm² cylinder), Rust **8 faces (two 314.159 mm² halves)**; in the green app (fd036a5c) Mass and section properties › Face on the hole wall reads **314.159 mm²** (tickets/rust-mirror-hole-wall-fd03.png); live merges it like OCCT. The split also leaves a seam circle inside the hole: mirhole has 15 edges with Rust (3 circular) vs 14 with OCCT (2), so the seam shows up as a pickable edge (a fillet of "all circular edges" takes it; volume unchanged). Any part mirrored onto itself through a hole / boss gets a split face (selection, fillet / chamfer chains, drawings' silhouettes). (qa 2026-10-28 23:15, build 5eb5259b)

## Lead / build
- [ ] P2 UPDATE-HELPER-LINUX-PARITY (lead; the same-app rule) the update helper behaves differently on Linux than the Windows one
  after 8b5fcd9c: (1) **no update-helper.log on Linux** — update-helper.sh logs only when NOSHAPE_UPDATE_LOG is set (the test
  harness's), so a real update leaves nothing beside the download (Windows: always logs there); (2) **other noshape copies are not
  closed** — a `noshape mcp` started from the install keeps running the replaced binary (/proc/<pid>/exe "(deleted)"), so an MCP
  client stays on the old version until it reconnects (Windows: closed first). Seen on vibe3's installed app, v0.4.30 → v0.4.31
  with v0.4.30's helper (the first update by it): the app ended on 0.4.31, g34 reopened, the mcp copy (pid 892613) still running,
  no log anywhere under ~/.config/noshape/updates (qa-scratch/upd431-0/-1.png). (qa 2026-11-05 11:17, v0.4.31)
- [ ] P1 GREEN-ICONS-PATH (lead, greens 4b9d7eaf and b8f46ab6): the green app shows **no icons at all** (toolbar, left rail, feature list, dialog ✓ / ✕, toasts, bottom-right controls; tickets/green-no-icons-4b9d.png) and logs thousands of `could not find asset at path "icons/part.svg"`. Cause: these greens were built in /home/ngyekta/projects/noshape-wt/watch (strings in the binary); 4994be03 and earlier in /root/projects/noshape-wt/watch. crates/app-core/src/assets.rs uses rust-embed 8 without `debug-embed`, so in this non-release profile the icons are read at run time from `<build tree>/crates/app/assets`, which doesn't exist on this box (`has_icon` fails → the `icons/part.svg` fallback fails too; i18n is embedded the same way). A symlink doesn't help (rust-embed canonicalizes and rejects it). qa's workaround: a copy of main's crates/app/assets at /home/ngyekta/projects/noshape-wt/watch/crates/app/assets (+ an empty crates/app-core dir) — icons back (0 errors), but stale when icons change. Fix: build the green where it runs, or enable rust-embed's `debug-embed` feature for the green profile. Release CI builds (release profile) embed the files, so the published app isn't affected (v0.4.12 smoke had icons). (qa 10-28 20:25)

- [ ] P2 DISPLACEMENT-TOP-VOLUME (core 11-05 12:18) a displacement map's replaced flat top (a 512² or 704² B-spline
  trimmed by the cylinder's circle) takes 20 s for its volume (mass properties; the side face's 0.6-1 s): common.cpp
  face_moments' boundary integral cuts the circle's curve on the face at every knot it crosses and runs an inner
  integral along u from u0 for each Gauss point, capped (20,000 segments a piece), so the time is flat in the size
  but large. Wants an area quadrature over the knot cells inside the trim instead, or inner integrals shared along
  the boundary. Repro: T2 model custom_features::displacement_map_scales (its "volume" column).

  core 11-03 05:30: **found**: the document's one Fillet (R12 on edge 16, NOSHAPE_DUMP_OPS fillet-0) alone crashes:
  OCCT's ChFi3d throws "TopoDS_UnCompatibleShapes: TopoDS_Builder::Add" partway and leaves **its input** broken (valid
  before, invalid after; freed list nodes in the input solid), and the next attempt (our retries at a hair smaller
  radius, the working scale, the fallbacks) or a later feature walks the freed topology. With MALLOC_PERTURB_ the
  fillet alone faults every run (kernel T2 blend::dumped_fillet_repeat_probe, FILLET_CASE). Fix: the fillet's, variable
  fillet's and chamfer's OCCT paths run on a copy of the cached body (the same sub-shapes in the same order, so the
  history's indices are the input's): a fault inside the failing ChFi3d is caught by the kernel's guard ("the kernel
  crashed"), the cached body stays intact, and the document regenerates without dying, 10 runs of 10 (was 2–5 of 5
  dying). (A copy per attempt was dropped: a history composed through a copy reports untouched faces Modified, which
  moves persistent names.) Next: the exact
  OCCT bug (TKFillet / TopOpeBRepBuild adding into the input), an OCCT patch.

  core 10-30 17:20: found and fixed (2feeebcb + a7023995, landed 20:12; CI run 41097497981 green on all three): not caches (the regen cache cut
  to 1 version grows the same, jemalloc the same) but budget jumps. On Linux a budget ended an op with the CPU
  timer's siglongjmp, which skips every destructor: each over-budget Shell Boolean left its pave filler and Extrema
  grids behind (each of the seed's 7 jumps +0.55–0.7 GB of live heap, measured between CLI requests with mallinfo2;
  heap profile: BOPAlgo_PaveFiller / Extrema_GenExtPS under op_shell_solid). Now a budget is first a deadline on the
  thread's CPU clock that OCCT stops at by throwing (the Boolean's progress indicator, patch 0007's hook in blends /
  Draft, new patch 0008's hook at each math_FunctionSetRoot solve, Shell's own Booleans now built with the
  indicator); the jump only after a grace (half the budget, at least 0.1 s) for code that checks neither. Seed 508 to
  step 85 on the dev CLI: 0 jumps, live heap 95 MB, peak RSS 1.1 GB (was 7.2 GB, 4.9 GB live, and OOM later).

Shots tickets/dialog-ok-empty-extrude-6011.png, dialog-ok-empty-mirror-6011.png, dialog-ok-invalid-expr-6011.png (qa 2026-10-27 14:20, green 6011065d) — **verified a6c56638** (qa 00:32 10-28: (a) Shift+E, Depth 30 Enter with nothing picked → ✓ enabled as live; (b) Fillet Radius "abc" / "1+" → pink field, ✓ disabled, field tip "Syntax error in expression.", title tip "Fillet 2 did not regenerate properly: A parameter value is not valid."; tickets/dialog-ok-syntax-a6c5.png)
  - psui (10-27): Onshape's bundle (woolsthorpe 425850a4): the dialog's `canSubmit()` is `!haveParamValidationError
    && !isExpressionValidating && isDirty && !createVariableDialogOpen`; the generic feature controller sets isDirty
    on every sendTreeEdit (any parameter edit) and on a regeneration without an error, which reads as (a) enabled
    after the Depth edit, as ours. Quantity parameters propagate their errors to the feature
    (`getDoParameterErrorsPropagateToFeature` true), so an invalid quantity would disable ✓, but behaviours 4290 says
    ✓ stays enabled for "abc". Both cases asked live (REQUESTS psui 10-27 DIALOG-OK-STATE (a), (b)); changed once
    answered.
  - qa (15:42, green 2a6d0167): live's own p1audit crops (onshape-research/live/p1audit/st-*.png, 68 states clicked in live) back psui's canSubmit reading for (a): **new Revolve, Surface tab clicked, nothing picked → live ✓ green**, ours pale (tickets/dialog-ok-revolve-surface-live-ours.png, live left); Revolve › Thin → both pale (dialog-ok-revolve-thin-live-ours.png). So my (a) was wrong as written: live does enable ✓ on an empty dialog once it's dirty; the difference left is that a tab switch doesn't make ours dirty (a Depth edit does). (b) stays with psui's live request.
  - psui (landed with this note): live (behaviours "psui (2026-10-27, P1) DIALOG-OK-STATE") = ours for (a): any edit
    makes the dialog dirty, ✓ enabled, a failing feature commits red; and for (b): an invalid value disables ✓ ("abc",
    "1+", "10 deg"). Fixed the differences: "1+" reads "Syntax error in expression." (was "Enter a valid
    expression."); a value out of its range is a parameter error too (✓ disabled, title "A parameter value is not
    valid.", a count's tip "Enter an integer value between 1 and 2500. Current value is 0."). Checked on :102.

- [x] P1 GPU-TEST-SEGV (lead 2026-10-27) GPU test binaries crash now and then with SIGSEGV inside libvulkan (lavapipe) on
  this machine: ops `--test all` (hidden_edges, per core) at 09:31 and render `--test all` (offscreen) at 12:13; the next
  run passes. The watcher retries T1 once on it (/tmp/lead/flaky.log counts them). Find the cause (Renderer/Instance
  creation per test vs one shared device, lavapipe thread-safety, a missing device wait before drop) and fix it so a
  GPU test never crashes the process.
  - psui (landed with this note): reproduced: the render test binary in three parallel loops died with SIGSEGV once
    in 27 runs (rc 139). Under gdb the crash is on the main thread in `__run_exit_handlers` after every test passed,
    jumping to unmapped code (`?? ()`) while a pipeline-compile thread is still up: an exit handler / destructor of a
    driver library (lavapipe's ICD or Mesa's EGL, both loaded per wgpu Instance) after that library was unloaded.
    Every `Renderer::new` made its own Instance + device and dropped it, so the loader loaded and unloaded the
    drivers for each test. Now `Renderer::new` / `new_async` use one process-wide adapter, device and queue
    (`renderer::shared_device`), made once and never dropped (the app's Metal-matching path keeps its own). After:
    90 runs in three parallel loops, no crash; the render tests take 24 s instead of 45. Stress test
    offscreen::renderers_on_many_threads_share_the_device (8 threads × 4 renderers). The ops crash (hidden_edges)
    renders through scene::png::Offscreen → Renderer::new, the same path.

    compound). The API takes the same two edges for the compound feature (feature_add OK), so it is the dialog's pick
    filter that rejects ALLOWS_AXIS. Live: the exact entity types per mode and for the custom query are asked in
    REQUESTS "qa (2026-10-27, P1 LP-DIRECTION-PICK)" (A)–(F) (qa 2026-10-27 10:10)
  - Likely cause of the compound-filter part (code read, qa 10:15): crates/custom/src/custom/studio.rs `filter_of`
    `Ann::And` intersects the two sides' bits. `EntityType.EDGE` = ANY_EDGE = EDGE | SKETCH_EDGE (params.rs:189), and
    `ALLOWS_AXIS` = LINEAR_EDGE | CIRCULAR_EDGE | SKETCH_EDGE | CYLINDRICAL_FACE | MATE_CONNECTOR (studio.rs:738), so the
    intersection is **SKETCH_EDGE only** and every model edge is rejected. The same holds for `EDGE && GeometryType.LINE`
    / `.CIRCLE` and `EDGE && ALLOWS_DIRECTION`: the "EDGE && LINE -> linear edges" refinement in the comment never
    yields model edges, because `EDGE` doesn't carry LINEAR_EDGE / CIRCULAR_EDGE (or SOLID_EDGE). The
    `std_edge_filters_match_builtin` test only covers std features that have a built-in twin, so it misses custom code.
  - Confirmed in the app (green e8858e61, qa 10:20; doc /root/qa-scratch/tickets/cfs2.noshape: the lpd part plus a line
    in Sketch 3 on Front, four custom features with 2-pick "Edges" fields). Clicking the part's top front edge and the
    sketch line:
    | Filter | part edge | sketch line |
    |---|---|---|
    | A `EDGE && ALLOWS_AXIS` | nothing | "Edge of Sketch 3" |
    | B `EDGE && GeometryType.LINE` | nothing | (not tried) |
    | C `EDGE && BodyType.SOLID` | "Edge of Extrude 1" | — |
    | D `ALLOWS_AXIS` alone | "Edge of Extrude 1" | — |
    B (`EDGE && GeometryType.LINE`) is probably the most common edge filter in custom features; `EDGE && CIRCLE`
    reduces the same way. Face filters are fine (`FACE && PLANE` keeps PLANAR_FACE, `FACE && CYLINDER` keeps
    CYLINDRICAL_FACE) since EntityType.FACE carries those bits. Screenshots:
    tickets/pick-green-custom-{A-axisedge-model-none,A-axisedge-sketch-only,B-edge-line-none,C-edge-solid-ok,D-allows-axis-ok}.png
  - psui: the v0.4.9 part is PREVIEW-NO-PICK (fixed 4e55b01f; render test a_preview_picks_the_edges_of_the_model_with_the_feature
    now covers edges too). The compound filters: custom/studio.rs `filter_of`'s `&&` takes any model edge (EDGE) as
    covering the linear and circular ones, so `EDGE && ALLOWS_AXIS` takes linear, circular and sketch edges, `EDGE &&
    GeometryType.LINE` linear and sketch edges, `EDGE && CIRCLE` circular ones (model test
    custom_features::compound_edge_filters_take_model_edges).

Left over, by design (a913de0e): once idle, the document thumbnail re-regenerates the head in the background ("7 features evaluated, 0 cached, 47455 ms", Fillet 2 burning its budget) and stops when the view regenerates again) reopened in part (qa 11:53, green e8858e61, NOSHAPE_PERF=1, fresh copy of cli-fillet-hang.noshape): the visible part
  is fixed: with the opening regen in Fillet 2 (crash marker "Fillet 2", +55 s), dragging the bar above Fillet 2 gives
  schedule_regen #2 → "0 evaluated, 7 cached, 1 ms" at once and the rolled-back model stays (tickets/regen-no-cancel-
  green-rolled.png). But the running run is **not aborted**: its marker stays and it finishes "8 features evaluated,
  0 cached, 84919 ms" 84 s later (tickets/regen-no-cancel-green-perf.txt), burning a core on a result nobody wants, and
  any edit that needs the kernel meanwhile shares it. The fix text says the newer run aborts the running one; the
  blend budget's cancel jump (97081557) evidently doesn't fire. Side note: a SIGTERM'd app mid-regen leaves its marker
  and the next open rolls the document back before that feature (DOC-POISON guard); Ctrl+Q clears it (reopens 8 / 8).
- [x] c0bbde51 P0 REGEN-NO-CANCEL fixed (psui 10-27) — **verified 2a6d0167** (qa 15:27: cli-fillet-hang.noshape copy, NOSHAPE_PERF=1, bar dragged above Shell 1 while the opening regen ran → "0 features evaluated, 6 cached, 0 ms" and the model at once; dropped above Fillet 2 instead it waits for Shell 1 itself, 47 s at load ~38, as slow on v0.4.10 (62.9 s): SHELL-SLOW, not this): a newer run that evaluates fewer features (the rollback bar above the running feature, a dialog on an earlier feature) aborts the running one; its finished features stay cached, so the rollback shows at once (app on cli-fillet-hang.noshape: 0 ms, 7 cached, while Fillet 2 ran). Was: a newer regeneration waits for a stale one instead of cancelling it: open a copy of
  /root/qa-scratch/tickets/cli-fillet-hang.noshape in green 502416cc (NOSHAPE_PERF=1); while the opening regen is still
  in Fillet 2 (it fails after the 30 s blend budget), drag the rollback bar above Fillet 2: schedule_regen #2 at 05:16:57,
  but its result (7 features, all cached, 1 ms) only arrives at 05:17:30, when Fillet 2's budget ran out; the spinner
  stays for 33 s for a rollback that costs nothing. The blend budget is cancellable (core 97081557: "jumps back when the
  budget is spent or the regeneration is cancelled"), but scheduling a new regen doesn't cancel the running one. Same
  shape as DRAFT-HANG's stuck app (there forever). PRIORITIES P0 "aborting stale work"; live never makes a cheap edit wait (qa 2026-10-27,
  green 502416cc)
  Likely cause (docs/handoff/kernel-robustness.md, gate (a)): `schedule_regen` aborts each running run "it doesn't
  extend"; a rollback above the running feature is a prefix of the running run, so it is treated as extended and waits
  for the whole run instead of taking the cached prefix at once.

    no thumbnail, the thumbnailer waited its 60 s for the busy view, then regenerated the whole head in parallel with
    its own Regenerator (0 cached) and no cancel. A thumbnail regeneration now stops when one of the document's Part
    Studio views starts regenerating (`start_thumbnail::regen_started` aborts it) and is made again once things settle.

  noshape window never gets X input focus, so it is never "active" and GPUI dispatches no focus / blur events at all
  (`Window::draw` sends empty focus paths for an inactive window): no input ever blurs, so nothing commits on Tab or a
  click away, while keys still arrive (xdotool sends them to the window under the pointer). With the window focused
  (`xdotool windowfocus <window id>` after the app starts) Tab commits and the preview regenerates at once, as live.
  **qa: give the app window X focus before testing anything focus-related** (blur, Tab, click-away commits, focus rings).
  Fixed alongside: a Tab commit scheduled the preview twice (psui, this commit).

  edge carries two association attributes, its definition edge's ("JAG", sheet at y −2) and one ("JAN" / "JAK") that
  also sits on an edge of the *other* definition sheet (z +2). Our attributes are kept by persistent Reference and
  transient ids are interned per Reference (custom host mod.rs `transient`), so the two sheets' edges most likely
  share a persistent name from the Thicken: every attribute std attaches to one lands on both. Next: query the two
  sheets' edge names after the Thicken; the fix is in naming (core) or in sheetMetalStart's per-region sheets.
  Second probe (all definition-sheet edges): every definition edge carries **two** association attributes (e.g. the
  junction edge [JAG, JAN]), where std's assignSMAssociationAttributes gives each entity one; the second is the
  other sheet's. Suspect: std's assign-to-new-or-split pass sees the entities as new again (our qAttributeQuery /
  qSubtraction of already-associated entities) and assigns a second id. Check that pass next.
  Root cause (11-01): not that pass. Each model's part names its new faces New(0..n) by order, so the parts of the
  two models (Thicken of two regions) had the same face names, hence the same edge names; attributes are kept by
  name, so std's association on one part's edge sat on the other's too. Fix: custom host sheetmetal.rs gives a
  part whose fresh names are taken already its own (Role::Copy with the definition / part index); a lone part keeps
  its names. Make joint on the junction edge now says live's "Cannot add joint joining selected entities."; T1
  sheetmetal_std::two_thickened_walls_name_their_parts_apart.

A legacy (native) plate's top face gives x = +X as live (GUI 10-30 on qa's smf.noshape): the face's plane comes from build_part's make_face_from_wire, whose x axis OCCT picks; changing it moves existing face sketches. Likely rule (skasm 10-30): the std plate's top face is a reversed face on the definition's −Z plane (x = +X); our face frame (eval.rs raw_plane ← kernel find_plane) flips both normal and x for it, Onshape flips the normal and keeps the surface's x (then y = n × x = +Y, live's frame). The same would hold for native extrudes' reversed (bottom) caps, so the fix is general: lead to decide (document compatibility). live (behaviours "skasm (2026-10-30, P3) a sketch's frame on a part's top face", request 1619): (40, 30, 2) on both; the sketch frame on a planar face = its plane with origin the world origin projected onto it, x = +X, y = +Y. (skasm 2026-10-30 17:10) skasm 10-30 fixed on the sheet metal side, no general frame change: the native extrude's reversed bottom cap already keeps its surface's x (find_plane flips only the normal); the std plate's top had x = −X because the host's wall rebuild fitted a new plane to the outline (make_face_from_wire: origin at the centroid, OCCT's axes). The walls are now made on the definition face's own plane and axes, so the top is x = +X, y = +Y as live; T2 library_circle_emboss_as_live sketches on the top face and checks the point lands at (40, 30, 2).

- [x] e7562f23 P3 SKETCH-DEGENERATE-SOLVE a dimension edit whose solution shrinks a line to a point fails: a 20 × 10 corner
  rectangle (corner at the origin, length 20, distance 10 to the virtual corner), top-right sketch fillet R6, radius
  set to R20 → "Sketch could not be solved.". Live (behaviours "skasm (2026-10-31) hole row name, unsolvable dimension
  colours" (b), psui/holerow/s-8) solves it: the arc's centre goes to (0, −10), the right edge runs down to y −10, the
  top line is a point. Setting the 10 to 0 also solves in live. Ours: the homotopy and the direct solve stall at max
  residual 4e-10 m (tolerance 1e-10). On the zero-length top line, Horizontal, Parallel, the tangent's Perpendicular
  and the virtual sharp's point-on-line are identically zero, which leaves the corner height free. The fix is a
  solver change: equations of a degenerate line keep their direction, or a stalled near-zero residual counts as solved.
  `collapsed_lines` (CON-HV-COLLAPSE) would also revert it; that rule should only apply when constraints cause the
  collapse, not a dimension value (skasm 2026-10-31)
  skasm 10-31 later: neither the stall rule (two steps < 1e-6 relative gain; 20 allowed → the same) nor the collapse
  revert (disabled → the same) is the blocker. The direct solve, the 4 / 16 / 64-step homotopies and the final solve all
  end at 4.002e-10 (the final one in 4 iterations), so the degenerate equations themselves need reworking.
  skasm 10-31 evening: sketch-crate reproduction in skasm-scratch/pend/degen_test.rs (bottom 20, left 10, fillet R6 → R20:
  stalls at 2.43e-10). With the tolerance at 1e-9 the solve reports Overconstrained [0, 1, 3, 7] and keeps the old shape,
  so the DOF / conflict analysis at the collapsed line also reads the rank loss as a conflict; both need the rework.
  Likely root: equations.rs writes Parallel / Perpendicular normalised (cross or dot / (|u| |v|)), and a fillet's
  tangency with a shared end is Perpendicular(centre→contact, line): as the top line shrinks to a point their gradients
  grow like 1/|u| and the line's direction is noise, so LM stalls. A fix: the unnormalised forms scaled by a fixed
  length (or dropping a collapsed line's direction equations); solver-wide, so check sk211 / fz211 timing and the
  constraint tests after it.
  skasm 10-31 fixed: Parallel / Perpendicular / point-on-line normalise by max(length, 1 µm) (equations.rs MIN_LEN), and
  dimension edits may collapse a line (SolveOptions::allow_collapse from dimension_edit_options; constraint edits keep
  CON-HV-COLLAPSE's refusal). R6 → R20: arc centre (0, −10), right edge to y −10, top line a point = live (CLI and T1
  tools::fillet_radius_past_the_rectangle_collapses_the_top_line); sketch + scene suites, sk211 / fz211 timing and fuzz
  1..40 pass.
  Live's "distance 10 set to 0" solves too (T1 tools::distance_set_to_zero_collapses_the_sides; CLI the same).

H / V and coincident-on-axis hints do show. Cause (code read): Tool::Line keeps `chain_end` None until the first segment exists (sketch_mode.rs ~2431), so `InferenceContext::anchor_point` is None and inference.rs's anchor-curve rules (~353–390) never run for a first segment; their unit test (inference.rs ~439) sets anchor_point itself. live (sketch.md 6.10, B§6): starting a line at an arc's end and moving along its tangent shows the tangent inference and adds Tangent (qa 2026-10-28 11:35, build ea572159)
  live answered (behaviours "qa (2026-10-28, P2) SKETCH-START-INFERENCE", request 1550): yes. A line started on an existing endpoint infers from the direction moved: Tangent (curves; arc highlighted orange, yellow-dashed rubber band, Tangent glyph at the cursor; stored as one TANGENT `<line>.endSnap0`, no COINCIDENT), Perpendicular / Parallel (lines; stored COINCIDENT + PERPENDICULAR), and Horizontal / Vertical win when the direction is axis-aligned.

  /root/qa-scratch/tickets/sk211-step89.noshape (Sketch 1: 273 entities — 209 points, 45 lines, 7 circles, 7 arcs, 3
  ellipses, **2 splines** — 97 constraints, dof 280; made by bin/skfuzz.py 211 89), bin/sk211time.py: Coincident
  point `:257` on spline `:215` → **175.6 s**, ends "over defined (conflicting constraints)"; then a dimension −10
  between points `:137` / `:48` → **295.1 s**, fails "Sketch could not be solved." (watcher CLI 12:08 ≥ green
  0d75aa8e, load ~45; the fuzz runs on CLI 09:50 hit 123–290 s on the same steps, 59 of 198 steps over 5 s). A
  backtrace taken mid-solve (tickets/sk211-solver-bt.txt): solver.rs solve_comp → eval → equations.rs:402
  `numeric_grad` → :327 eval → :317 `fit_curve` → nurbs.rs:374 `interpolate` (an LU solve): the numeric gradient
  re-interpolates the fit spline for every perturbed variable, so each Jacobian costs O(vars × spline solve). Live
  solves such edits at once; in the app this would freeze the sketch for minutes (P0 "responsiveness while editing").
  Analytic derivatives for the fit-spline equations (or re-fit once per Newton step, not per variable), and a solve
  time budget that reports failure fast (qa 12:30, CLI 12:08; v0.4.10 timing running)
  skasm 10-27 (f34c32be): the fit spline equations (point on spline, end directions, curvature) have their own
  gradients from the fit's factored, banded system (O(n) a value instead of O(n²) fits a Jacobian row). Same sketch in
  the sketch crate (dev build, load 35–50): the Coincident 68 s → 0.3–0.7 s, the −10 dimension after it 0.2–0.4 s, both
  still "over defined" as before. T1 test sketch solve_time::sk211_constraints_on_a_94_point_fit_spline_solve_quickly
  skasm 10-27 15:05: also 0b0f1abd (regions with long splines 36× faster) and allocation-free spline evaluation: qa's
  bin/skfuzz.py 211 200 on the dev CLI: slowest step 0.74 s (was up to 290 s), mean 0.34 s a step at 300–440 entities
  (each step includes a full sketch_get); sk211time.py: Coincident 0.8 s, the dimension 0.5 s
